## Phase 1. READING: Understanding the Problem<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me carefully analyze the issue description:

1. The problem occurs when parsing Jinja templates with SQL
2. There's a specific error: "Length of templated file mismatch with final slice: 286 != 58"
3. The problematic SQL contains:
   - A set statement defining max_partitions = 5
   - A for loop iterating over range(max_partitions)
   - Multiple set statements inside the loop (useless1 through useless5)
   - A SELECT statement with a WHERE clause using the loop variable
   - An endfor statement

Key technical details:
- Error message: "Length of templated file mismatch with final slice: 286 != 58"
- Command: sqlfluff lint test.sql --dialect ansi
- Version: sqlfluff 3.0.7, python 3.9
- The issue seems to be related to template slicing/parsing logic

The problem appears to be in the Jinja templating engine integration where there's a mismatch between the expected length of the templated output and the actual sliced output. This suggests an issue with how SQLFluff handles template slicing when there are multiple Jinja statements in sequence.

Steps to reproduce:
1. Create a SQL file with the provided Jinja template
2. Run sqlfluff lint on it with ANSI dialect
3. Observe the warning about length mismatch

Best practices for fixing:
- Need to understand how template slicing works in SQLFluff
- Should preserve the existing functionality while fixing the slice calculation

- Should test with various combinations of Jinja statements</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me reword the problem clearly:

### 1.1 Problem Analysis
The issue involves a **Jinja template parsing error** in SQLFluff where there's a mismatch between expected and actual template lengths during slicing operations.

### 1.2 Technical Details Highlighted
- **Error message**: "Length of templated file mismatch with final slice: 286 != 58"
- **Method/context**: Template slicing in Jinja processing
- **File type**: SQL files with Jinja templates
- **Command**: `sqlfluff lint test.sql --dialect ansi`
- **Variables**: `max_partitions`, loop variable `i`, multiple `useless` variables

### 1.3 Clear Problem Statement
SQLFluff fails to correctly calculate template slices when processing Jinja templates that contain multiple consecutive `{% set %}` statements within a `{% for %}` loop. This causes a length mismatch warning during the linting process.

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce
1. Create a SQL file containing the problematic Jinja template
2. Run `sqlfluff lint test.sql --dialect ansi`
3. Observe the warning about template length mismatch

### 1.5 Best Practices for Testing/Fixing
- Understand the template slicing mechanism in SQLFluff
- Preserve existing functionality while fixing the slice calculation
- Test with various Jinja statement combinations
- Ensure the fix handles edge cases properly

    35	- [Db2](https://www.ibm.com/analytics/db2)
    36	- [DuckDB](https://duckdb.org/)

     1	# SQLFluff - Contributing
     2	
     3	:star2: **First** - thanks for being interested in improving SQLFluff! :smiley:
     4	
     5	:star2: **Second** - please read and familiarise yourself with both the content
     6	of this guide and also our [code of conduct](CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md).
     7	
     8	:star2: **Third** - the best way to get started contributing, is to use the
     9	tool in anger and then to submit bugs and features through GitHub.
    10	In particular, in helping to develop the parser, examples of queries
    11	that do not parse as expected are especially helpful.
    12	
    13	:star2: **Fourth** - making sure that our documentation is up-to-date and useful
    14	for new users is really important. If you are a new user, you are in precisely
    15	the best position to do this. Familiarise yourself with the tool (as per step
    16	2 above) and familiarise yourself with the current documentation (live version
    17	at [docs.sqlfluff.com](https://docs.sqlfluff.com) and the source can be found
    18	in the [docs](./docs/) folder of the repo). Pull requests are always welcome
    19	with documentation improvements. Keep in mind that there are linting checks in
    20	place for good formatting so keep an eye on the tests whenever submitting a PR.
    21	We also have a [GitHub wiki](https://github.com/sqlfluff/sqlfluff/wiki) for
    22	longer tutorials. We welcome
    23	[contributions, suggestions or requests](https://github.com/sqlfluff/sqlfluff/issues/2104)
    24	for the wiki.
    25	
    26	:star2: **Fifth** - if you are so inclined - pull requests on the core codebase
    27	are always welcome. Dialect additions are often a good entry point for new
    28	contributors, and we have
    29	[a wiki page](https://github.com/sqlfluff/sqlfluff/wiki/Contributing-Dialect-Changes)
    30	to help you through your first contribution. Bear in mind that all the tests
    31	should pass, and test coverage should not decrease unduly as part of the
    32	changes which you make. You may find it useful to familiarise yourself with the
    33	[architectural principles here](https://docs.sqlfluff.com/en/stable/perma/architecture.html)
    34	and with the [current documentation here](https://docs.sqlfluff.com).
    35	
    36	## How The Community Works
    37	
    38	SQLFluff is maintained by a community of volunteers, which means we have a
    39	few processes in place to allow everyone to contribute at a level that suits
    40	them and at a time that suits them. These are not meant to be a way of restricting
    41	development, but a way of allowing the community to agree what to focus on
    42	and then effectively direct its focus toward that. Anyone can pipe up in these
    43	discussions, and the more we hear from users the more we can build a tool
    44	that is useful for the community.
    45	
    46	- Large features for consideration will be organised into _Major Releases_.
    47	  These will usually include significant changes in functionality or backwards-incompatible
    48	  changes. As some of these features may require significant
    49	  coordination, discussion or development work, there is a process for each
    50	  major release to work out what features will fit into that release.
    51	  - Each major release will have its own GitHub issue. For example, the link
    52	    to the issue for [0.6.0 is here](https://github.com/sqlfluff/sqlfluff/issues/922).
    53	  - Features or issues are organised into a _shortlist_. During the initial
    54	    discussion for the release, each feature is vetted for enough clarity
    55	    that someone in the community can pick it up. Issues, where we cannot
    56	    reach clarity, will be pushed to the next release. Getting this clarity
    57	    is important before development work progresses so that we know that
    58	    larger changes are a) in line with the aims of the project and b) are
    59	    effectively pre-approved changes so that there are not any surprises
    60	    when it comes to merging.
    61	  - Once we reach the deadline for closing the roadmap for a release the
    62	    focus on development work should be on those features.
    63	- Small features and bug fixes (assuming no backward compatibility issues)
    64	  do not need to go through the same process and vetting and can be picked
    65	  up and merged at any time.
    66	
    67	### Maintainers
    68	
    69	A small group of people volunteer their time to maintain the project and
    70	share the responsibility for responding to issues and reviewing any proposed
    71	changes via pull requests. Each one of them will be trying to follow
    72	the process above and keep development work on the project moving. That
    73	means for smaller changes and improvements they may review changes as
    74	individuals and merge them into the project in a very lightweight way.
    75	For larger changes, especially if not already part of the current major
    76	release process the expectation is that they will involve other members
    77	or the maintainer community or the project admins before merging in
    78	larger changes or giving the green light to major structural project
    79	changes.
    80

    84	
    85	#### Requirements
    86	
    87	The simplest way to set up a development environment is to use `tox`.
    88	First ensure that you have tox installed:
    89	```shell
    90	python3.8 -m pip install -U tox
    91	```
    92	**IMPORTANT:** `tox` must be installed with a minimum of Python 3.8 as
    93	the `mypy` checks are incompatible with 3.7. Those using newer versions of
    94	Python may replace `python3.8` as necessary (the test suite runs primarily
    95	under 3.12 for example).
    96	
    97	Note: Unfortunately tox does not currently support setting just a minimum
    98	Python version (though this may be be coming in tox 4!).

    15	def iter_indices_of_newlines(raw_str: str) -> Iterator[int]:
    16	    """Find the indices of all newlines in a string."""
    17	    init_idx = -1
    18	    while True:
    19	        nl_pos = raw_str.find("\n", init_idx + 1)
    20	        if nl_pos >= 0:
    21	            yield nl_pos
    22	            init_idx = nl_pos
    23	        else:
    24	            break  # pragma: no cover TODO?

    27	def large_file_check(func):
    28	    """Raise an exception if the file is over a defined size.
    29	
    30	    Designed to be implemented as a decorator on `.process()` methods.
    31	
    32	    If no config is provided or the relevant config value is set
    33	    to zero then the check is skipped.
    34	    """
    35	
    36	    def _wrapped(
    37	        self, *, in_str: str, fname: str, config: Optional[FluffConfig] = None, **kwargs
    38	    ):
    39	        if config:
    40	            limit = config.get("large_file_skip_char_limit")
    41	            if limit:
    42	                templater_logger.warning(
    43	                    "The config value large_file_skip_char_limit was found set. "
    44	                    "This feature will be removed in a future release, please "
    45	                    "use the more efficient 'large_file_skip_byte_limit' instead."
    46	                )
    47	            if limit and len(in_str) > limit:
    48	                raise SQLFluffSkipFile(
    49	                    f"Length of file {fname!r} is over {limit} characters. "
    50	                    "Skipping to avoid parser lock. Users can increase this limit "
    51	                    "in their config by setting the 'large_file_skip_char_limit' "
    52	                    "value, or disable by setting it to zero."
    53	                )
    54	        return func(self, in_str=in_str, fname=fname, config=config, **kwargs)
    55	
    56	    return _wrapped

    59	class RawFileSlice(NamedTuple):
    60	    """A slice referring to a raw file."""
    61	
    62	    raw: str  # Source string
    63	    slice_type: str
    64	    source_idx: int  # Offset from beginning of source string
    65	    # Block index, incremented on start or end block tags, e.g. "if", "for".
    66	    # This is used in `BaseRule.discard_unsafe_fixes()` to reject any fixes
    67	    # which span multiple templated blocks.
    68	    block_idx: int = 0
    69	    # The command of a templated tag, e.g. "if", "for"
    70	    # This is used in template tracing as a kind of cache to identify the kind
    71	    # of template element this is without having to re-extract it each time.
    72	    tag: Optional[str] = None
    73	
    74	    def end_source_idx(self) -> int:
    75	        """Return the closing index of this slice."""
    76	        return self.source_idx + len(self.raw)
    77	
    78	    def source_slice(self) -> slice:
    79	        """Return a slice object for this slice."""
    80	        return slice(self.source_idx, self.end_source_idx())
    81	
    82	    def is_source_only_slice(self) -> bool:
    83	        """Based on its slice_type, does it only appear in the *source*?
    84	
    85	        There are some slice types which are automatically source only.
    86	        There are *also* some which are source only because they render
    87	        to an empty string.
    88	        """
    89	        # TODO: should any new logic go here?
    90	        return self.slice_type in ("comment", "block_end", "block_start", "block_mid")

   114	class TemplatedFile:
   115	    """A templated SQL file.
   116	
   117	    This is the response of a templaters .process() method
   118	    and contains both references to the original file and also
   119	    the capability to split up that file when lexing.
   120	    """
   121	
   122	    def __init__(
   123	        self,
   124	        source_str: str,
   125	        fname: str,
   126	        templated_str: Optional[str] = None,
   127	        sliced_file: Optional[List[TemplatedFileSlice]] = None,
   128	        raw_sliced: Optional[List[RawFileSlice]] = None,
   129	    ):
   130	        """Initialise the TemplatedFile.
   131	
   132	        If no templated_str is provided then we assume that the file is NOT
   133	        templated and that the templated view is the same as the source view.
   134	
   135	        Args:
   136	            source_str (str): The source string.
   137	            fname (str): The file name.
   138	            templated_str (Optional[str], optional): The templated string.
   139	                Defaults to None.
   140	            sliced_file (Optional[List[TemplatedFileSlice]], optional): The sliced file.
   141	                Defaults to None.
   142	            raw_sliced (Optional[List[RawFileSlice]], optional): The raw sliced file.
   143	                Defaults to None.
   144	        """
   145	        self.source_str = source_str
   146	        # An empty string is still allowed as the templated string.
   147	        self.templated_str = source_str if templated_str is None else templated_str
   148	        # If no fname, we assume this is from a string or stdin.
   149	        self.fname = fname
   150	        # Assume that no sliced_file, means the file is not templated
   151	        self.sliced_file: List[TemplatedFileSlice]
   152	        if sliced_file is None:
   153	            if self.templated_str != self.source_str:  # pragma: no cover
   154	                raise ValueError("Cannot instantiate a templated file unsliced!")
   155	            # If we get here and we don't have sliced files,
   156	            # then it's raw, so create them.
   157	            self.sliced_file = [
   158	                TemplatedFileSlice(
   159	                    "literal", slice(0, len(source_str)), slice(0, len(source_str))
   160	                )
   161	            ]
   162	            assert (
   163	                raw_sliced is None
   164	            ), "Templated file was not sliced, but not has raw slices."
   165	            self.raw_sliced: List[RawFileSlice] = [
   166	                RawFileSlice(source_str, "literal", 0)
   167	            ]
   168	        else:
   169	            self.sliced_file = sliced_file
   170	            assert raw_sliced is not None, "Templated file was sliced, but not raw."
   171	            self.raw_sliced = raw_sliced
   172	
   173	        # Precalculate newlines, character positions.
   174	        self._source_newlines = list(iter_indices_of_newlines(self.source_str))
   175	        self._templated_newlines = list(iter_indices_of_newlines(self.templated_str))
   176	
   177	        # Consistency check raw string and slices.
   178	        pos = 0
   179	        rfs: RawFileSlice
   180	        for rfs in self.raw_sliced:
   181	            assert rfs.source_idx == pos, (
   182	                "TemplatedFile. Consistency fail on running source length"
   183	                f": {pos} != {rfs.source_idx}"
   184	            )
   185	            pos += len(rfs.raw)
   186	        assert pos == len(self.source_str), (
   187	            "TemplatedFile. Consistency fail on total source length"
   188	            f": {pos} != {len(self.source_str)}"
   189	        )
   190	
   191	        # Consistency check templated string and slices.
   192	        previous_slice = None
   193	        tfs: Optional[TemplatedFileSlice] = None
   194	        for tfs in self.sliced_file:
   195	            if previous_slice:
   196	                if tfs.templated_slice.start != previous_slice.templated_slice.stop:
   197	                    raise SQLFluffSkipFile(  # pragma: no cover
   198	                        "Templated slices found to be non-contiguous. "
   199	                        f"{tfs.templated_slice} (starting"
   200	                        f" {self.templated_str[tfs.templated_slice]!r})"
   201	                        f" does not follow {previous_slice.templated_slice} "
   202	                        "(starting "
   203	                        f"{self.templated_str[previous_slice.templated_slice]!r}"
   204	                        ")"
   205	                    )
   206	            else:
   207	                if tfs.templated_slice.start != 0:
   208	                    raise SQLFluffSkipFile(  # pragma: no cover
   209	                        "First Templated slice not started at index 0 "
   210	                        f"(found slice {tfs.templated_slice})"
   211	                    )
   212	            previous_slice = tfs
   213	        if self.sliced_file and templated_str is not None:
   214	            if tfs.templated_slice.stop != len(templated_str):
   215	                raise SQLFluffSkipFile(  # pragma: no cover
   216	                    "Length of templated file mismatch with final slice: "
   217	                    f"{len(templated_str)} != {tfs.templated_slice.stop}."
   218	                )

   220	    @classmethod
   221	    def from_string(cls, raw: str) -> "TemplatedFile":
   222	        """Create TemplatedFile from a string."""
   223	        return cls(source_str=raw, fname="<string>")
   224	
   225	    def __repr__(self) -> str:  # pragma: no cover TODO?
   226	        """Return a string representation of the 'TemplatedFile' object."""
   227	        return "<TemplatedFile>"

   229	    def __str__(self) -> str:

   233	    def get_line_pos_of_char_pos(
   234	        self, char_pos: int, source: bool = True
   235	    ) -> Tuple[int, int]:
   236	        """Get the line number and position of a point in the source file.
   237	
   238	        Args:
   239	            char_pos: The character position in the relevant file.
   240	            source: Are we checking the source file (as opposed to the
   241	                templated file)
   242	
   243	        Returns:
   244	            line_number, line_position
   245	
   246	        """
   247	        if source:
   248	            ref_str = self._source_newlines
   249	        else:
   250	            ref_str = self._templated_newlines
   251	
   252	        nl_idx = bisect_left(ref_str, char_pos)
   253	
   254	        if nl_idx > 0:
   255	            return nl_idx + 1, char_pos - ref_str[nl_idx - 1]
   256	        else:
   257	            # NB: line_pos is char_pos+1 because character position is 0-indexed,
   258	            # but the line position is 1-indexed.
   259	            return 1, char_pos + 1
   260	
   261	    def _find_slice_indices_of_templated_pos(
   262	        self,
   263	        templated_pos: int,
   264	        start_idx: Optional[int] = None,
   265	        inclusive: bool = True,
   266	    ) -> Tuple[int, int]:
   267	        """Find a subset of the sliced file which touch this point.
   268	
   269	        NB: the last_idx is exclusive, as the intent is to use this as a slice.
   270	        """
   271	        start_idx = start_idx or 0
   272	        first_idx = None
   273	        last_idx = start_idx
   274	        # Work through the sliced file, starting at the start_idx if given
   275	        # as an optimisation hint. The sliced_file is a list of TemplatedFileSlice
   276	        # which reference parts of the templated file and where they exist in the
   277	        # source.
   278	        for idx, elem in enumerate(self.sliced_file[start_idx:]):
   279	            last_idx = idx + start_idx
   280	            if elem[2].stop >= templated_pos:
   281	                if first_idx is None:
   282	                    first_idx = idx + start_idx
   283	                if elem[2].start > templated_pos:
   284	                    break
   285	                elif not inclusive and elem[2].start >= templated_pos:
   286	                    break
   287	        # If we got to the end add another index
   288	        else:
   289	            last_idx += 1
   290	        if first_idx is None:  # pragma: no cover
   291	            raise ValueError("Position Not Found")
   292	        return first_idx, last_idx
   293	
   294	    def raw_slices_spanning_source_slice(
   295	        self, source_slice: slice
   296	    ) -> List[RawFileSlice]:
   297	        """Return a list of the raw slices spanning a set of indices."""
   298	        # Special case: The source_slice is at the end of the file.
   299	        last_raw_slice = self.raw_sliced[-1]
   300	        if source_slice.start >= last_raw_slice.source_idx + len(last_raw_slice.raw):
   301	            return []
   302	        # First find the start index
   303	        raw_slice_idx = 0
   304	        # Move the raw pointer forward to the start of this patch
   305	        while (
   306	            raw_slice_idx + 1 < len(self.raw_sliced)
   307	            and self.raw_sliced[raw_slice_idx + 1].source_idx <= source_slice.start
   308	        ):
   309	            raw_slice_idx += 1
   310	        # Find slice index of the end of this patch.
   311	        slice_span = 1
   312	        while (
   313	            raw_slice_idx + slice_span < len(self.raw_sliced)
   314	            and self.raw_sliced[raw_slice_idx + slice_span].source_idx
   315	            < source_slice.stop
   316	        ):
   317	            slice_span += 1
   318	        # Return the raw slices:
   319	        return self.raw_sliced[raw_slice_idx : raw_slice_idx + slice_span]

   321	    def templated_slice_to_source_slice(
   322	        self,
   323	        template_slice: slice,
   324	    ) -> slice:
   325	        """Convert a template slice to a source slice."""
   326	        if not self.sliced_file:
   327	            return template_slice  # pragma: no cover TODO?
   328	
   329	        ts_start_sf_start, ts_start_sf_stop = self._find_slice_indices_of_templated_pos(
   330	            template_slice.start
   331	        )
   332	
   333	        ts_start_subsliced_file = self.sliced_file[ts_start_sf_start:ts_start_sf_stop]
   334	
   335	        # Work out the insertion point
   336	        insertion_point = -1
   337	        for elem in ts_start_subsliced_file:
   338	            # Do slice starts and ends:
   339	            for slice_elem in ("start", "stop"):
   340	                if getattr(elem[2], slice_elem) == template_slice.start:
   341	                    # Store the lowest.
   342	                    point = getattr(elem[1], slice_elem)
   343	                    if insertion_point < 0 or point < insertion_point:
   344	                        insertion_point = point
   345	                    # We don't break here, because we might find ANOTHER
   346	                    # later which is actually earlier.
   347	
   348	        # Zero length slice.
   349	        if template_slice.start == template_slice.stop:
   350	            # Is it on a join?
   351	            if insertion_point >= 0:
   352	                return zero_slice(insertion_point)
   353	            # It's within a segment.
   354	            else:
   355	                if (
   356	                    ts_start_subsliced_file
   357	                    and ts_start_subsliced_file[0][0] == "literal"
   358	                ):
   359	                    offset = template_slice.start - ts_start_subsliced_file[0][2].start
   360	                    return zero_slice(
   361	                        ts_start_subsliced_file[0][1].start + offset,
   362	                    )
   363	                else:
   364	                    raise ValueError(  # pragma: no cover
   365	                        "Attempting a single length slice within a templated section! "
   366	                        f"{template_slice} within {ts_start_subsliced_file}."
   367	                    )
   368	
   369	        # Otherwise it's a slice with length.
   370	
   371	        # Use a non inclusive match to get the end point.
   372	        ts_stop_sf_start, ts_stop_sf_stop = self._find_slice_indices_of_templated_pos(
   373	            template_slice.stop, inclusive=False
   374	        )
   375	
   376	        # Update starting position based on insertion point:
   377	        if insertion_point >= 0:
   378	            for elem in self.sliced_file[ts_start_sf_start:]:
   379	                if elem[1].start != insertion_point:
   380	                    ts_start_sf_start += 1
   381	                else:
   382	                    break
   383	
   384	        subslices = self.sliced_file[
   385	            # Very inclusive slice
   386	            min(ts_start_sf_start, ts_stop_sf_start) : max(
   387	                ts_start_sf_stop, ts_stop_sf_stop
   388	            )
   389	        ]
   390	        if ts_start_sf_start == ts_start_sf_stop:
   391	            if ts_start_sf_start > len(self.sliced_file):  # pragma: no cover
   392	                # We should never get here
   393	                raise ValueError("Starting position higher than sliced file position")
   394	            if ts_start_sf_start < len(self.sliced_file):  # pragma: no cover
   395	                return self.sliced_file[1].source_slice
   396	            else:
   397	                return self.sliced_file[-1].source_slice  # pragma: no cover
   398	        else:
   399	            start_slices = self.sliced_file[ts_start_sf_start:ts_start_sf_stop]
   400	        if ts_stop_sf_start == ts_stop_sf_stop:  # pragma: no cover TODO?

   743	    def slice_file(
   744	        self, raw_str: str, render_func: Callable[[str], str], config=None, **kwargs
   745	    ) -> Tuple[List[RawFileSlice], List[TemplatedFileSlice], str]:
   746	        """Slice the file to determine regions where we can fix.
   747	
   748	        Args:
   749	            raw_str (str): The raw string to be sliced.
   750	            render_func (Callable[[str], str]): The rendering function to be used.
   751	            config (optional): Optional configuration.
   752	            **kwargs: Additional keyword arguments.
   753	
   754	        Returns:
   755	            Tuple[List[RawFileSlice], List[TemplatedFileSlice], str]:
   756	                A tuple containing a list of raw file slices, a list of
   757	                templated file slices, and the templated string.
   758	        """
   759	        # The JinjaTracer slicing algorithm is more robust, but it requires
   760	        # us to create and render a second template (not raw_str).
   761	
   762	        templater_logger.info("Slicing File Template")
   763	        templater_logger.debug("    Raw String: %r", raw_str[:80])
   764	        analyzer = self._get_jinja_analyzer(raw_str, self._get_jinja_env())
   765	        tracer = analyzer.analyze(render_func)
   766	        trace = tracer.trace(append_to_templated=kwargs.pop("append_to_templated", ""))
   767	        return trace.raw_sliced, trace.sliced_file, trace.templated_str

   769	    @staticmethod
   770	    def _rectify_templated_slices(
   771	        length_deltas: Dict[int, int], sliced_template: List[TemplatedFileSlice]
   772	    ):
   773	        """This method rectifies the source slices of a variant template.
   774	
   775	        :TRICKY: We want to yield variants that _look like_ they were
   776	        rendered from the original template. However, they were actually
   777	        rendered from a modified template, which means they have source
   778	        indices which won't line up with the source files. We correct that
   779	        here by using the length deltas generated earlier from the
   780	        modifications.
   781	
   782	        This should ensure that lint issues and fixes for the variants are
   783	        handled correctly and can be combined with those from the original
   784	        template.
   785	        """
   786	        # NOTE: We sort the stack because it's important that it's in order
   787	        # because we're going to be popping from one end of it. There's no
   788	        # guarantee that the items are in a particular order a) because it's
   789	        # a dict and b) because they may have been generated out of order.
   790	        delta_stack = sorted(length_deltas.items(), key=lambda t: t[0])

    66	    def trace(
    67	        self,
    68	        append_to_templated: str = "",
    69	    ) -> JinjaTrace:
    70	        """Executes raw_str. Returns template output and trace."""
    71	        trace_template_str = "".join(
    72	            (
    73	                cast(str, self.raw_slice_info[rs].alternate_code)
    74	                if self.raw_slice_info[rs].alternate_code is not None
    75	                else rs.raw
    76	            )
    77	            for rs in self.raw_sliced
    78	        )
    79	        trace_template_output = self.render_func(trace_template_str)
    80	        # Split output by section. Each section has two possible formats.
    81	        trace_entries: List[regex.Match[str]] = list(
    82	            regex.finditer(r"\0", trace_template_output)
    83	        )
    84	        # If the file has no templated entries, we should just iterate
    85	        # through the raw slices to add all the placeholders.
    86	        if not trace_entries:
    87	            for raw_idx, _ in enumerate(self.raw_sliced):
    88	                self.record_trace(0, raw_idx)
    89	
    90	        for match_idx, match in enumerate(trace_entries):
    91	            pos1 = match.span()[0]
    92	            try:
    93	                pos2 = trace_entries[match_idx + 1].span()[0]
    94	            except IndexError:
    95	                pos2 = len(trace_template_output)
    96	            p = trace_template_output[pos1 + 1 : pos2]
    97	            m_id = regex.match(r"^([0-9a-f]+)(_(\d+))?", p)
    98	            if not m_id:
    99	                raise ValueError(  # pragma: no cover
   100	                    "Internal error. Trace template output does not match expected "
   101	                    "format."
   102	                )
   103	            if m_id.group(3):
   104	                # E.g. "00000000000000000000000000000001_83". The number after
   105	                # "_" is the length (in characters) of a corresponding literal
   106	                # in raw_str.
   107	                alt_id, slice_length = m_id.group(1), int(m_id.group(3))
   108	            else:
   109	                # E.g. "00000000000000000000000000000002 a < 10". The characters
   110	                # after the slice ID are executable code from raw_str.
   111	                alt_id, slice_length = m_id.group(0), len(p[len(m_id.group(0)) + 1 :])
   112	
   113	            target_slice_idx = self.find_slice_index(alt_id)
   114	            target_inside_block = self.raw_slice_info[
   115	                self.raw_sliced[target_slice_idx]
   116	            ].inside_block
   117	            if not target_inside_block:
   118	                # Normal case: Walk through the template.
   119	                self.move_to_slice(target_slice_idx, slice_length)
   120	            else:

   134	    def find_slice_index(self, slice_identifier: Union[int, str]) -> int:
   135	        """Given a slice identifier, return its index.
   136	
   137	        A slice identifier is a string like 00000000000000000000000000000002.
   138	        """
   139	        raw_slices_search_result = [
   140	            idx
   141	            for idx, rs in enumerate(self.raw_sliced)
   142	            if self.raw_slice_info[rs].unique_alternate_id == slice_identifier
   143	        ]
   144	        if len(raw_slices_search_result) != 1:
   145	            raise ValueError(  # pragma: no cover
   146	                f"Internal error. Unable to locate slice for {slice_identifier}."
   147	            )
   148	        return raw_slices_search_result[0]
   149	
   150	    def move_to_slice(
   151	        self,
   152	        target_slice_idx: int,
   153	        target_slice_length: int,
   154	    ) -> Dict[int, List[int]]:
   155	        """Given a template location, walk execution to that point.
   156	
   157	        This updates the internal `program_counter` to the appropriate
   158	        location.
   159	
   160	        Returns:
   161	            :obj:`dict`: For each step in the template, a :obj:`list` of
   162	                which steps are accessible. In many cases each step will
   163	                only have one accessible next step (the following one),
   164	                however for branches in the program there may be more than
   165	                one.
   166	        """
   167	        step_candidates = {}
   168	        while self.program_counter < len(self.raw_sliced):
   169	            self.record_trace(
   170	                target_slice_length if self.program_counter == target_slice_idx else 0
   171	            )
   172	            current_raw_slice = self.raw_sliced[self.program_counter]
   173	            if self.program_counter == target_slice_idx:
   174	                # Reached the target slice. Go to next location and stop.
   175	                self.program_counter += 1
   176	                break
   177	
   178	            # Choose the next step.
   179	            # We could simply go to the next slice (sequential execution).
   180	            candidates = [self.program_counter + 1]

   203	        slice_idx: Optional[int] = None,

   205	    ) -> None:
   206	        """Add the specified (default: current) location to the trace.
   207	
   208	        Args:

   231	            )
   232	        )
   233	        if target_slice_length:
   234	            self.source_idx += target_slice_length

   237	@dataclass(frozen=True)
   238	class JinjaTagConfiguration:
   239	    """Provides information about a Jinja tag and how it affects JinjaAnalyzer behavior.
   240	
   241	    Attributes:
   242	        block_type (str): The block type that the Jinja tag maps to; eventually stored
   243	            in TemplatedFileSlice.slice_type and RawFileSlice.slice_type.

   247	            execution path through the block.

   250	            through the block and record a potential backward jump to the loop

   526	    }
   527	
   528	    def analyze(self, render_func: Callable[[str], str]) -> JinjaTracer:
   529	        """Slice template in jinja."""
   530	        # str_buff and str_parts are two ways we keep track of tokens received
   531	        # from Jinja. str_buff concatenates them together, while str_parts
   532	        # accumulates the individual strings. We generally prefer using
   533	        # str_parts. That's because Jinja doesn't just split on whitespace, so
   534	        # by keeping tokens as Jinja returns them, the code is more robust.
   535	        # Consider the following:
   536	        #   {% set col= "col1" %}
   537	        # Note there's no space after col. Jinja splits this up for us. If we
   538	        # simply concatenated the parts together and later split on whitespace,
   539	        # we'd need some ugly, fragile logic to handle various whitespace
   540	        # possibilities:
   541	        #   {% set col= "col1" %}
   542	        #   {% set col = "col1" %}
   543	        #   {% set col ="col1" %}
   544	        # By using str_parts and letting Jinja handle this, it just works.
   545	
   546	        str_buff = ""
   547	        str_parts = []
   548	
   549	        # https://jinja.palletsprojects.com/en/2.11.x/api/#jinja2.Environment.lex
   550	        block_idx = 0
   551	        for _, elem_type, raw in self.env.lex(self.raw_str):
   552	            if elem_type == "data":
   553	                self.track_literal(raw, block_idx)
   554	                continue
   555	            str_buff += raw
   556	            str_parts.append(raw)
   557	
   558	            if elem_type.endswith("_begin"):
   559	                self.handle_left_whitespace_stripping(raw, block_idx)
   560	
   561	            raw_slice_info: RawSliceInfo = self.make_raw_slice_info(None, None)
   562	            tag_contents = []
   563	            # raw_end and raw_begin behave a little differently in
   564	            # that the whole tag shows up in one go rather than getting
   565	            # parts of the tag at a time.
   566	            m_open = None
   567	            m_close = None
   568	            if elem_type.endswith("_end") or elem_type == "raw_begin":
   569	                block_type = self.block_types[elem_type]
   570	                block_tag = None
   571	                # Handle starts and ends of blocks
   572	                if block_type in ("block", "templated"):
   573	                    m_open = self.re_open_tag.search(str_parts[0])
   574	                    m_close = self.re_close_tag.search(str_parts[-1])
   575	                    if m_open and m_close:
   576	                        tag_contents = self.extract_tag_contents(
   577	                            str_parts, m_close, m_open, str_buff
   578	                        )
   579	
   580	                    if block_type == "block" and tag_contents:
</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me look at the make_raw_slice_info method:<tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__3.1 && grep -n "def make_raw_slice_info" src/sqlfluff/core/templaters/slicers/tracer.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>501:    def make_raw_slice_info(

Let me now create a better debug script that shows the slicing details:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__3.1/debug_slices.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

"""Debug script to examine the slicing behavior."""

from sqlfluff.core.templaters.jinja import JinjaTemplater
from sqlfluff.core.config import FluffConfig

# Test SQL content
test_sql = """{% set max_partitions = 5 %}
{% for i in range(max_partitions) %}{% set useless1 = 0 %}{% set useless2 = 0 %}{% set useless3 = 0 %}{% set useless4 = 0 %}{% set useless5 = 0 %}
    SELECT * FROM my_table
    WHERE partition_key = {{ i }};
{% endfor %}"""

def main():
    print("=== Debugging Jinja Slicing ===")
    print("Input SQL:")
    print(repr(test_sql))
    print()
    
    # Create templater and config
    templater = JinjaTemplater()
    config = FluffConfig.load_config_at_path(".")
    
    # Process the template
    try:
        result, violations = templater.process(
            in_str=test_sql,
            fname="test.sql",
            config=config
        )
        
        print(f"Templated string length: {len(result.templated_str)}")
        print(f"Templated string: {repr(result.templated_str)}")
        print()
        
        print("Raw slices:")
        for i, raw_slice in enumerate(result.raw_sliced):
            print(f"  {i}: {raw_slice}")
        print()
        
        print("Templated slices:")
        for i, templated_slice in enumerate(result.sliced_file):
            print(f"  {i}: {templated_slice}")
            if i < len(result.sliced_file) - 1:
                expected_start = templated_slice.templated_slice.stop
                next_actual_start = result.sliced_file[i+1].templated_slice.start
                if expected_start != next_actual_start:
                    print(f"    *** GAP/Overlap: expected next start {expected_start}, got {next_actual_start}")
        print()
        
        if result.sliced_file:
            last_slice = result.sliced_file[-1]
            print(f"Last templated slice stop: {last_slice.templated_slice.stop}")
            print(f"Actual templated string length: {len(result.templated_str)}")
            
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()

if __name__ == "__main__":

Now let me modify the debug script to catch the error and inspect the slices before the error occurs:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__3.1/debug_slices.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

"""Debug script to examine the slicing behavior."""

from sqlfluff.core.templaters.jinja import JinjaTemplater
from sqlfluff.core.config import FluffConfig
from sqlfluff.core.errors import SQLFluffSkipFile

# Test SQL content
test_sql = """{% set max_partitions = 5 %}
{% for i in range(max_partitions) %}{% set useless1 = 0 %}{% set useless2 = 0 %}{% set useless3 = 0 %}{% set useless4 = 0 %}{% set useless5 = 0 %}
    SELECT * FROM my_table
    WHERE partition_key = {{ i }};
{% endfor %}"""

def main():
    print("=== Debugging Jinja Slicing ===")
    print("Input SQL:")
    print(repr(test_sql))
    print()
    
    # Create templater and config
    templater = JinjaTemplater()
    config = FluffConfig(overrides={"dialect": "ansi"})
    
    # Process the template but catch the error to inspect slices
    try:
        # Get the raw slices and templated slices before creating the TemplatedFile
        from sqlfluff.core.templaters.jinja import JinjaTemplater
        
        # Manually call slice_file to get the intermediate results
        env, live_context, render_func = templater.construct_render_func(
            fname="test.sql", config=config
        )
        
        raw_sliced, sliced_file, out_str = templater.slice_file(
            test_sql,
            render_func=render_func,
            config=config,
        )
        
        print(f"Templated string length: {len(out_str)}")
        print(f"Templated string: {repr(out_str)}")
        print()
        
        print("Raw slices:")
        for i, raw_slice in enumerate(raw_sliced):
            print(f"  {i}: {raw_slice}")
        print()
        
        print("Templated slices:")
        for i, templated_slice in enumerate(sliced_file):
            print(f"  {i}: {templated_slice}")
            if i < len(sliced_file) - 1:
                expected_start = templated_slice.templated_slice.stop
                next_actual_start = sliced_file[i+1].templated_slice.start
                if expected_start != next_actual_start:
                    print(f"    *** GAP/Overlap: expected next start {expected_start}, got {next_actual_start}")
        print()
        
        if sliced_file:
            last_slice = sliced_file[-1]
            print(f"Last templated slice stop: {last_slice.templated_slice.stop}")
            print(f"Actual templated string length: {len(out_str)}")
            if last_slice.templated_slice.stop != len(out_str):
                print(f"*** MISMATCH DETECTED: {last_slice.templated_slice.stop} != {len(out_str)}")
            
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error during slicing: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()

if __name__ == "__main__":

"""Debug script to examine the slicing behavior."""

from sqlfluff.core.templaters.jinja import JinjaTemplater
from sqlfluff.core.config import FluffConfig

# Test SQL content
test_sql = """{% set max_partitions = 5 %}
{% for i in range(max_partitions) %}{% set useless1 = 0 %}{% set useless2 = 0 %}{% set useless3 = 0 %}{% set useless4 = 0 %}{% set useless5 = 0 %}
    SELECT * FROM my_table
    WHERE partition_key = {{ i }};
{% endfor %}"""

def main():
    print("=== Debugging Jinja Slicing ===")
    print("Input SQL:")
    print(repr(test_sql))
    print()
    
    # Create templater and config
    templater = JinjaTemplater()
    config = FluffConfig(overrides={"dialect": "ansi"})
    
    # Process the template
    try:
        result, violations = templater.process(
            in_str=test_sql,
            fname="test.sql",
            config=config
        )
        
        print(f"Templated string length: {len(result.templated_str)}")
        print(f"Templated string: {repr(result.templated_str)}")
        print()
        
        print("Raw slices:")
        for i, raw_slice in enumerate(result.raw_sliced):
            print(f"  {i}: {raw_slice}")
        print()
        
        print("Templated slices:")
        for i, templated_slice in enumerate(result.sliced_file):
            print(f"  {i}: {templated_slice}")
            if i < len(result.sliced_file) - 1:
                expected_start = templated_slice.templated_slice.stop
                next_actual_start = result.sliced_file[i+1].templated_slice.start
                if expected_start != next_actual_start:
                    print(f"    *** GAP/Overlap: expected next start {expected_start}, got {next_actual_start}")
        print()
        
        if result.sliced_file:
            last_slice = result.sliced_file[-1]
            print(f"Last templated slice stop: {last_slice.templated_slice.stop}")
            print(f"Actual templated string length: {len(result.templated_str)}")
            
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Understanding the Problem<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me analyze what's happening:

1. The Jinja template has a for loop that iterates 5 times
2. Inside the loop, there are multiple set statements that render to nothing (0 length)
3. Then there's literal SQL that renders to actual content
4. The tracer correctly identifies that the loop executes and generates content
5. BUT the slicing logic only accounts for the first iteration

Looking at the templated slices:
- Slice 8 covers the literal SQL and says it goes from templated position 1 to 55
- Slice 9 covers the {{ i }} variable and says it goes from 55 to 56  
- Slice 10 covers the ;\n and says it goes from 56 to 58

But there should be 5 copies of this pattern, so the final slice should end at position 286, not 58.

The problem is in the JinjaTracer.move_to_slice method. When it encounters a loop, it needs to account for all the iterations, not just the first one.

Looking at the code in tracer.py around lines 150-180, the move_to_slice method walks through the program counter and records traces. But when it encounters a loop that may repeat, it's not properly calculating the total length contributed by all iterations.

The issue seems to be that the tracer simulation only simulates one pass through the loop, but the actual rendering executes the loop multiple times. So the templated slice lengths are calculated based on one iteration, but the actual templated string contains multiple iterations.

   150	    def move_to_slice(
   151	        self,
   152	        target_slice_idx: int,
   153	        target_slice_length: int,
   154	    ) -> Dict[int, List[int]]:
   155	        """Given a template location, walk execution to that point.
   156	
   157	        This updates the internal `program_counter` to the appropriate
   158	        location.
   159	
   160	        Returns:
   161	            :obj:`dict`: For each step in the template, a :obj:`list` of
   162	                which steps are accessible. In many cases each step will
   163	                only have one accessible next step (the following one),
   164	                however for branches in the program there may be more than
   165	                one.
   166	        """
   167	        step_candidates = {}
   168	        while self.program_counter < len(self.raw_sliced):
   169	            self.record_trace(
   170	                target_slice_length if self.program_counter == target_slice_idx else 0
   171	            )
   172	            current_raw_slice = self.raw_sliced[self.program_counter]
   173	            if self.program_counter == target_slice_idx:
   174	                # Reached the target slice. Go to next location and stop.
   175	                self.program_counter += 1
   176	                break
   177	
   178	            # Choose the next step.
   179	            # We could simply go to the next slice (sequential execution).
   180	            candidates = [self.program_counter + 1]
   181	            # If we have other options, consider those.
   182	            candidates.extend(
   183	                filter(
   184	                    # They're a valid possibility if
   185	                    # they don't take us past the target.
   186	                    lambda idx: idx <= target_slice_idx,
   187	                    self.raw_slice_info[current_raw_slice].next_slice_indices,
   188	                )
   189	            )
   190	            # Choose the candidate that takes us closest to the target.
   191	            candidates.sort(key=lambda c: abs(target_slice_idx - c))
   192	            # Save all the candidates for each step so we can return them later.
   193	            step_candidates[self.program_counter] = candidates
   194	            # Step forward to the best step found.
   195	            self.program_counter = candidates[0]
   196	
   197	        # Return the candidates at each step.
   198	        return step_candidates
   199

"""Debug script to examine the simpler slicing behavior."""

from sqlfluff.core.templaters.jinja import JinjaTemplater
from sqlfluff.core.config import FluffConfig

# Test SQL content - simpler version
test_sql = """{% set max_partitions = 2 %}
{% for i in range(max_partitions) %}{% set useless1 = 0 %}{% set useless2 = 0 %}{% set useless3 = 0 %}{% set useless4 = 0 %}{% set useless5 = 0 %}
    SELECT * FROM my_table
    WHERE partition_key = {{ i }};
{% endfor %}"""

def main():
    print("=== Debugging Simpler Jinja Slicing ===")
    print("Input SQL:")
    print(repr(test_sql))
    print()
    
    # Create templater and config
    templater = JinjaTemplater()
    config = FluffConfig(overrides={"dialect": "ansi"})
    
    # Process the template but catch the error to inspect slices
    try:
        # Manually call slice_file to get the intermediate results
        env, live_context, render_func = templater.construct_render_func(
            fname="test.sql", config=config
        )
        
        raw_sliced, sliced_file, out_str = templater.slice_file(
            test_sql,
            render_func=render_func,
            config=config,
        )
        
        print(f"Templated string length: {len(out_str)}")
        print(f"Templated string: {repr(out_str)}")
        print()
        
        print("Raw slices:")
        for i, raw_slice in enumerate(raw_sliced):
            print(f"  {i}: {raw_slice}")
        print()
        
        print("Templated slices:")
        for i, templated_slice in enumerate(sliced_file):
            print(f"  {i}: {templated_slice}")
            if i < len(sliced_file) - 1:
                expected_start = templated_slice.templated_slice.stop
                next_actual_start = sliced_file[i+1].templated_slice.start
                if expected_start != next_actual_start:
                    print(f"    *** GAP/Overlap: expected next start {expected_start}, got {next_actual_start}")
        print()
        
        if sliced_file:
            last_slice = sliced_file[-1]

   426	        (e.g. "{%- endif -%}"), because fewer breadcrumbs means JinjaTracer has

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now I understand the flow better. When the analyzer processes a literal, it calls `slice_info_for_literal(len(raw), "")` which creates an `alternate_code` like `\0{id}_{length}`. This alternate code gets embedded in the trace template.

When the trace template is rendered, it produces output where each slice's contribution is marked with these IDs and lengths. The tracer then parses this output to determine how much content each slice produced.

The critical issue is that for slices inside loops, the trace template output correctly shows the TOTAL length contributed by that slice across ALL iterations. But when the tracer walks through the raw slices to create the `TemplatedFileSlice` objects, it only records that total length once, instead of properly distributing it across all the places where that slice contributes content.

Let me examine this more closely. The key is in how `move_to_slice` works. When it encounters slice index 8 (the literal SQL), the `target_slice_length` parameter contains the total length (54 characters for our simple test case with 2 iterations), but it only records this once.

But wait, that's not quite right either. Let me look at what happens in the trace method more carefully. The trace method processes each trace entry and calls `move_to_slice` for each one. But if a slice contributes content in multiple iterations, shouldn't there be multiple trace entries for it?

Let me check if there are indeed multiple trace entries. I'll enhance the debug script to show the trace template output:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__3.1/debug_trace.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

"""Debug script to examine the trace template generation."""

from sqlfluff.core.templaters.jinja import JinjaTemplater
from sqlfluff.core.config import FluffConfig
import regex

# Test SQL content - simpler version
test_sql = """{% set max_partitions = 2 %}
{% for i in range(max_partitions) %}{% set useless1 = 0 %}{% set useless2 = 0 %}{% set useless3 = 0 %}{% set useless4 = 0 %}{% set useless5 = 0 %}
    SELECT * FROM my_table
    WHERE partition_key = {{ i }};
{% endfor %}"""

def main():
    print("=== Debugging Trace Template Generation ===")
    print("Input SQL:")
    print(repr(test_sql))
    print()
    
    # Create templater and config
    templater = JinjaTemplater()
    config = FluffConfig(overrides={"dialect": "ansi"})
    
    # Manually construct analyzer and tracer to examine internals
    from sqlfluff.core.templaters.slicers.tracer import JinjaAnalyzer
    
    env, live_context, render_func = templater.construct_render_func(
        fname="test.sql", config=config
    )
    
    analyzer = JinjaAnalyzer(test_sql, env)
    tracer = analyzer.analyze(render_func)
    
    # Show trace template
    trace_template_str = "".join(
        (
            tracer.raw_slice_info[rs].alternate_code
            if tracer.raw_slice_info[rs].alternate_code is not None
            else rs.raw
        )
        for rs in tracer.raw_sliced
    )
    
    print("Trace template string:")
    print(repr(trace_template_str))
    print()
    
    # Show trace template output
    trace_template_output = render_func(trace_template_str)
    print("Trace template output:")
    print(repr(trace_template_output))
    print()
    
    # Parse trace entries
    trace_entries = list(regex.finditer(r"\0", trace_template_output))
    print(f"Number of trace entries: {len(trace_entries)}")
    
    for match_idx, match in enumerate(trace_entries):
        pos1 = match.span()[0]
        try:
            pos2 = trace_entries[match_idx + 1].span()[0]
        except IndexError:
            pos2 = len(trace_template_output)
        p = trace_template_output[pos1 + 1 : pos2]
        m_id = regex.match(r"^([0-9a-f]+)(_(\d+))?", p)
        if m_id:
            if m_id.group(3):
                alt_id, slice_length = m_id.group(1), int(m_id.group(3))
                print(f"  Entry {match_idx}: ID={alt_id}, length={slice_length}")
            else:
                alt_id, slice_length = m_id.group(0), len(p[len(m_id.group(0)) + 1 :])
                print(f"  Entry {match_idx}: ID={alt_id}, length={slice_length} (code)")
        else:
            print(f"  Entry {match_idx}: Could not parse {p}")
    
    print()
    
    # Show mapping of IDs to slices
    print("ID to slice mapping:")
    for i, raw_slice in enumerate(tracer.raw_sliced):
        slice_info = tracer.raw_slice_info[raw_slice]
        if slice_info.unique_alternate_id:
            print(f"  Slice {i} (ID {slice_info.unique_alternate_id}): {raw_slice}")

if __name__ == "__main__":

[Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Excellent! Now I can see exactly what's happening:

1. **Trace template string**: This is what gets created by replacing each slice with its tracing information
2. **Trace template output**: This is what gets rendered when the trace template is processed  

Looking at the trace template output:
- Entry 0: Slice with ID ending in 0000 contributes 1 character (`\n`)
- Entry 1: Slice with ID ending in 0001 contributes 54 characters (first iteration of SQL)  
- Entry 2: Slice with ID ending in 0002 contributes 1 character (`0` from `{{ i }}`)
- Entry 3: Slice with ID ending in 0003 contributes 2 characters (`;\n`)
- Entry 4: Slice with ID ending in 0001 contributes 54 characters AGAIN (second iteration of SQL)
- Entry 5: Slice with ID ending in 0002 contributes 1 character AGAIN (`1` from `{{ i }}`) 
- Entry 6: Slice with ID ending in 0003 contributes 2 characters AGAIN (`;\n`)
- Entry 7: Slice with ID ending in 0004 contributes 0 characters (`{% endfor %}`)

So the trace correctly shows that:
- Slice 8 (the literal SQL) contributes 54 characters in BOTH iterations (entries 1 and 4)  
- Slice 9 (the `{{ i }}`) contributes 1 character in BOTH iterations (entries 2 and 5)
- Slice 10 (the `;\n`) contributes 2 characters in BOTH iterations (entries 3 and 6)

This totals to: 54 + 1 + 2 + 54 + 1 + 2 = 114 characters (plus the 1 from slice 1 = 115 total).

But when the tracer processes these entries in the `trace` method, it calls `move_to_slice` for each entry. The problem is that `move_to_slice` treats each call independently and doesn't realize that some slices contribute content multiple times.

Looking at the ID mappings:
- Slice 8 (ID ...0001) = the literal SQL = 54 chars × 2 iterations = should contribute 108 chars total
- Slice 9 (ID ...0002) = the `{{ i }}` = 1 char × 2 iterations = should contribute 2 chars total  
- Slice 10 (ID ...0003) = the `;\n` = 2 chars × 2 iterations = should contribute 4 chars total

But `move_to_slice` records each contribution separately instead of accumulating them.

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Clear Problem Statement

### 5.1 Problem Statement
The Jinja tracer correctly identifies that slices inside loops contribute content multiple times (as evidenced by the trace template output showing duplicate IDs with their respective lengths). However, the `move_to_slice` method in `JinjaTracer` records each contribution separately instead of accumulating the total length for slices that are executed multiple times.

### 5.2 Problem Location
The issue is in the `move_to_slice` method in `/workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__3.1/src/sqlfluff/core/templaters/slicers/tracer.py` around lines 150-198.

### 5.3 How the Test Reproduces the Issue
The test case has a for loop that iterates twice, with multiple set statements followed by literal SQL content. The trace correctly shows that the literal SQL slice contributes 54 characters in each iteration (total 108), but the slicing logic only records 54 characters total.

### 5.4 Best Practices for the Fix
- Preserve existing functionality for non-loop scenarios
- Properly accumulate lengths for slices executed multiple times  
- Maintain the existing tracing logic and data structures
- Ensure the fix handles nested loops and complex control flow

### 5.5 How to Fix the Problem
The fix needs to modify the tracer logic to accumulate the `target_slice_length` for slices that are encountered multiple times. Instead of calling `record_trace` with the individual length each time, we need to sum up the lengths for repeated slices and record the total.

Looking at the trace method, for each trace entry, it calls either `move_to_slice(target_slice_idx, slice_length)` or `record_trace(slice_length, target_slice_idx)`. We need to modify this to accumulate lengths for repeated slice indices.

        # (e.g., inside loops)
        slice_lengths: Dict[int, int] = {}
        slice_indices: List[int] = []
        
        for match_idx, match in enumerate(trace_entries):
            pos1 = match.span()[0]
            try:
                pos2 = trace_entries[match_idx + 1].span()[0]
            except IndexError:
                pos2 = len(trace_template_output)
            p = trace_template_output[pos1 + 1 : pos2]
            m_id = regex.match(r"^([0-9a-f]+)(_(\d+))?", p)
            if not m_id:
                raise ValueError(  # pragma: no cover
                    "Internal error. Trace template output does not match expected "
                    "format."
                )
            if m_id.group(3):
                # E.g. "00000000000000000000000000000001_83". The number after
                # "_" is the length (in characters) of a corresponding literal
                # in raw_str.
                alt_id, slice_length = m_id.group(1), int(m_id.group(3))
            else:
                # E.g. "00000000000000000000000000000002 a < 10". The characters
                # after the slice ID are executable code from raw_str.
                alt_id, slice_length = m_id.group(0), len(p[len(m_id.group(0)) + 1 :])

            target_slice_idx = self.find_slice_index(alt_id)
            
            # Accumulate the length for this slice index
            if target_slice_idx in slice_lengths:
                slice_lengths[target_slice_idx] += slice_length
            else:
                slice_lengths[target_slice_idx] = slice_length
                slice_indices.append(target_slice_idx)

                self.raw_sliced[target_slice_idx]
            ].inside_block

[Command finished with exit code 1]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>My fix broke some existing tests! The issue is that I'm accumulating lengths for ALL slices, but some slices should only be recorded once. My approach is too aggressive. 

Looking at the failing test case:
```
SELECT {# A comment #} {{field}} {% for i in [1, 3, 7]%}, fld_{{i}}_x{% endfor %} FROM my_schema.{{my_table}}
```

This has a loop that executes 3 times, so the slice `, fld_{{i}}_x` should contribute 3 times its length. But other slices like the initial `SELECT` part should only contribute once.

The problem is that I'm accumulating ALL slice lengths, but I should only accumulate lengths for slices that are actually inside loops that execute multiple times.

Let me examine one of the failing tests more closely. Looking at the error, it seems like slices outside the loop are also getting accumulated incorrectly.

Let me revert my change and think of a more targeted approach:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__3.1/src/sqlfluff/core/templaters/slicers/tracer.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>        for match_idx, match in enumerate(trace_entries):

            if not m_id:

                    "format."
                )
            if m_id.group(3):
                # E.g. "00000000000000000000000000000001_83". The number after
                # "_" is the length (in characters) of a corresponding literal
                # in raw_str.
                alt_id, slice_length = m_id.group(1), int(m_id.group(3))

            if target_slice_idx in slice_lengths:

        # multiple times (e.g., inside loops)
        slice_counts: Dict[int, int] = {}
        slice_lengths_by_occurrence: Dict[int, List[int]] = {}
        
        # First pass: count occurrences and collect lengths
        trace_data = []
        for match_idx, match in enumerate(trace_entries):
            pos1 = match.span()[0]
            try:
                pos2 = trace_entries[match_idx + 1].span()[0]
            except IndexError:
                pos2 = len(trace_template_output)
            p = trace_template_output[pos1 + 1 : pos2]
            m_id = regex.match(r"^([0-9a-f]+)(_(\d+))?", p)
            if not m_id:
                raise ValueError(  # pragma: no cover
                    "Internal error. Trace template output does not match expected "
                    "format."
                )
            if m_id.group(3):
                # E.g. "00000000000000000000000000000001_83". The number after
                # "_" is the length (in characters) of a corresponding literal
                # in raw_str.
                alt_id, slice_length = m_id.group(1), int(m_id.group(3))
            else:
                # E.g. "00000000000000000000000000000002 a < 10". The characters
                # after the slice ID are executable code from raw_str.
                alt_id, slice_length = m_id.group(0), len(p[len(m_id.group(0)) + 1 :])

            target_slice_idx = self.find_slice_index(alt_id)
            
            # Count occurrences
            slice_counts[target_slice_idx] = slice_counts.get(target_slice_idx, 0) + 1
            
            # Store lengths by occurrence
            if target_slice_idx not in slice_lengths_by_occurrence:
                slice_lengths_by_occurrence[target_slice_idx] = []
            slice_lengths_by_occurrence[target_slice_idx].append(slice_length)
            
            trace_data.append((target_slice_idx, slice_length, alt_id))
        
        # Second pass: process slices with appropriate lengths
        for target_slice_idx, slice_length, alt_id in trace_data:
            target_inside_block = self.raw_slice_info[
                self.raw_sliced[target_slice_idx]
            ].inside_block
            
            # For slices that appear multiple times, use the total accumulated length
            # only for the LAST occurrence to avoid double-counting
            if slice_counts[target_slice_idx] > 1:
                # Check if this is the last occurrence of this slice
                occurrences_so_far = sum(1 for ts_idx, _, _ in trace_data[:trace_data.index((target_slice_idx, slice_length, alt_id))+1] 
                                       if ts_idx == target_slice_idx)
                if occurrences_so_far < slice_counts[target_slice_idx]:
                    # Not the last occurrence, use length 0 to avoid double counting
                    adjusted_length = 0
                else:
                    # Last occurrence, use the total accumulated length
                    adjusted_length = sum(slice_lengths_by_occurrence[target_slice_idx])
            else:
                # Single occurrence, use the original length
                adjusted_length = slice_length
                
            if not target_inside_block:
                # Normal case: Walk through the template.
                self.move_to_slice(target_slice_idx, adjusted_length)
            else:
                # {% block %} executes code elsewhere in the template but does
                # not move there. It's a bit like macro invocation.

Actually, let me step back and think about the real problem. The issue isn't that I need to accumulate ALL lengths. The issue is that when `move_to_slice` is called multiple times for the same slice index, it should accumulate the lengths rather than overwrite them.

But looking at `move_to_slice`, it doesn't seem to be designed to handle this. Each call to `move_to_slice` advances the program counter and records a trace.

Let me look at this from a different angle. The real issue might be that I should modify the `record_trace` method or the way slices are accumulated.

Actually, let me try a much simpler approach. The key insight is that for slices that appear multiple times, I should only call `move_to_slice` once with the total accumulated length, not multiple times.

Let me simplify:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__3.1/src/sqlfluff/core/templaters/slicers/tracer.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>        # Group trace entries by slice index to handle slices that appear multiple times
        grouped_trace_data: Dict[int, List[Tuple[int, str]]] = {}
        
        # First pass: group trace entries by slice index
        for match_idx, match in enumerate(trace_entries):
            pos1 = match.span()[0]
            try:
                pos2 = trace_entries[match_idx + 1].span()[0]
            except IndexError:
                pos2 = len(trace_template_output)
            p = trace_template_output[pos1 + 1 : pos2]

                # in raw_str.

                # after the slice ID are executable code from raw_str.

            if target_slice_idx not in grouped_trace_data:
                grouped_trace_data[target_slice_idx] = []
            grouped_trace_data[target_slice_idx].append((slice_length, alt_id))

        for target_slice_idx, entries in grouped_trace_data.items():

                self.raw_sliced[target_slice_idx]

   137	                # {% block %} executes code elsewhere in the template but does
   138	                # not move there. It's a bit like macro invocation.
   139	                self.record_trace(total_length, target_slice_idx)
   140	
   141	        # TRICKY: The 'append_to_templated' parameter is only used by the dbt
   142	        # templater, passing "\n" for this parameter if we need to add one back.
   143	        # (The Jinja templater does not pass this parameter, so
   144

    def test__templater_jinja_slice_file(
        raw_file, override_context, result, templater_class, caplog
    ):
        """Test slice_file."""
        templater = templater_class(override_context=override_context)
        _, _, render_func = templater.construct_render_func(
            config=FluffConfig.from_path(
                "test/fixtures/templater/jinja_slice_template_macros"
            )
        )

        with caplog.at_level(logging.DEBUG, logger="sqlfluff.templater"):
            raw_sliced, sliced_file, templated_str = templater.slice_file(
                raw_file, render_func=render_func
            )
        # Create a TemplatedFile from the results. This runs some useful sanity
        # checks.
        _ = TemplatedFile(raw_file, "<<DUMMY>>", templated_str, sliced_file, raw_sliced)
        # Check contiguous on the TEMPLATED VERSION
        print(sliced_file)
        prev_slice = None
        for elem in sliced_file:
            print(elem)
            if prev_slice:
                assert elem[2].start == prev_slice.stop
            prev_slice = elem[2]
        # Check that all literal segments have a raw slice
        for elem in sliced_file:
            if elem[0] == "literal":
                assert elem[1] is not None
        # check result

            # Track contributions for non-zero lengths
            if slice_length > 0:
                if target_slice_idx not in slice_contributions:
                    slice_contributions[target_slice_idx] = []
                slice_contributions[target_slice_idx].append(slice_length)
        
        # Process trace entries
        for target_slice_idx, slice_length, alt_id in trace_entries_data:
            target_inside_block = self.raw_slice_info[
                self.raw_sliced[target_slice_idx]
            ].inside_block
            
            # Use original logic for most cases
            if not target_inside_block:
                # Normal case: Walk through the template.
                self.move_to_slice(target_slice_idx, slice_length)
            else:
                # {% block %} executes code elsewhere in the template but does
                # not move there. It's a bit like macro invocation.
                self.record_trace(slice_length, target_slice_idx)</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>        # Process trace entries - preserve original behavior for most cases
        # but handle the specific case where slices inside loops contribute
        # content multiple times
        slice_contribution_counts: Dict[int, int] = {}
        
        # First pass: count how many times each slice contributes content
        for match_idx, match in enumerate(trace_entries):
            pos1 = match.span()[0]
            try:
                pos2 = trace_entries[match_idx + 1].span()[0]
            except IndexError:
                pos2 = len(trace_template_output)
            p = trace_template_output[pos1 + 1 : pos2]
            m_id = regex.match(r"^([0-9a-f]+)(_(\d+))?", p)
            if not m_id:
                raise ValueError(  # pragma: no cover
                    "Internal error. Trace template output does not match expected "
                    "format."
                )
            if m_id.group(3):
                # E.g. "00000000000000000000000000000001_83". The number after
                # "_" is the length (in characters) of a corresponding literal
                # in raw_str.
                alt_id, slice_length = m_id.group(1), int(m_id.group(3))
            else:
                # E.g. "00000000000000000000000000000002 a < 10". The characters
                # after the slice ID are executable code from raw_str.
                alt_id, slice_length = m_id.group(0), len(p[len(m_id.group(0)) + 1 :])

            target_slice_idx = self.find_slice_index(alt_id)
            
            # Count contributions (non-zero lengths)
            if slice_length > 0:
                slice_contribution_counts[target_slice_idx] = \
                    slice_contribution_counts.get(target_slice_idx, 0) + 1

        # Second pass: process entries with adjusted logic for accumulated slices
        for match_idx, match in enumerate(trace_entries):
            pos1 = match.span()[0]
            try:
                pos2 = trace_entries[match_idx + 1].span()[0]
            except IndexError:
                pos2 = len(trace_template_output)
            p = trace_template_output[pos1 + 1 : pos2]
            m_id = regex.match(r"^([0-9a-f]+)(_(\d+))?", p)
            if not m_id:
                raise ValueError(  # pragma: no cover
                    "Internal error. Trace template output does not match expected "
                    "format."
                )
            if m_id.group(3):
                # E.g. "00000000000000000000000000000001_83". The number after
                # "_" is the length (in characters) of a corresponding literal
                # in raw_str.
                alt_id, slice_length = m_id.group(1), int(m_id.group(3))
            else:
                # E.g. "00000000000000000000000000000002 a < 10". The characters
                # after the slice ID are executable code from raw_str.
                alt_id, slice_length = m_id.group(0), len(p[len(m_id.group(0)) + 1 :])

            target_slice_idx = self.find_slice_index(alt_id)
            target_inside_block = self.raw_slice_info[
                self.raw_sliced[target_slice_idx]
            ].inside_block
            
            # For slices that contribute content multiple times due to loops,
            # we need special handling to ensure proper length accounting
            if slice_contribution_counts.get(target_slice_idx, 1) > 1 and slice_length > 0:
                # This slice contributes content multiple times. 
                # Check if this is the last contribution of this slice.
                # Count how many contributions we've seen so far.
                contributions_seen = 0
                for prev_match_idx in range(match_idx):
                    prev_pos1 = trace_entries[prev_match_idx].span()[0]
                    try:
                        prev_pos2 = trace_entries[prev_match_idx + 1].span()[0]
                    except IndexError:
                        prev_pos2 = len(trace_template_output)
                    prev_p = trace_template_output[prev_pos1 + 1 : prev_pos2]
                    prev_m_id = regex.match(r"^([0-9a-f]+)(_(\d+))?", prev_p)
                    if prev_m_id:
                        if prev_m_id.group(3):
                            prev_alt_id = prev_m_id.group(1)
                        else:
                            prev_alt_id = prev_m_id.group(0)
                        prev_target_slice_idx = self.find_slice_index(prev_alt_id)
                        if prev_target_slice_idx == target_slice_idx and (
                            (prev_m_id.group(3) and int(prev_m_id.group(3)) > 0) or
                            (not prev_m_id.group(3) and len(prev_p[len(prev_m_id.group(0)) + 1:]) > 0)
                        ):
                            contributions_seen += 1
                
                expected_contributions = slice_contribution_counts[target_slice_idx]
                
                if contributions_seen == expected_contributions - 1:
                    # This is the last contribution - use accumulated length instead
                    # Find all previous contributions of this slice to sum them up
                    accumulated_length = slice_length
                    for prev_match_idx in range(match_idx):
                        prev_pos1 = trace_entries[prev_match_idx].span()[0]
                        try:
                            prev_pos2 = trace_entries[prev_match_idx + 1].span()[0]
                        except IndexError:
                            prev_pos2 = len(trace_template_output)
                        prev_p = trace_template_output[prev_pos1 + 1 : prev_pos2]
                        prev_m_id = regex.match(r"^([0-9a-f]+)(_(\d+))?", prev_p)
                        if prev_m_id:
                            if prev_m_id.group(3):
                                prev_alt_id = prev_m_id.group(1)
                                prev_length = int(prev_m_id.group(3))
                            else:
                                prev_alt_id = prev_m_id.group(0)
                                prev_length = len(prev_p[len(prev_m_id.group(0)) + 1:])
                            prev_target_slice_idx = self.find_slice_index(prev_alt_id)
                            if prev_target_slice_idx == target_slice_idx:
                                accumulated_length += prev_length
                    
                    adjusted_length = accumulated_length
                elif contributions_seen == 0:
                    # First contribution - process normally but we'll handle the accumulation later
                    adjusted_length = slice_length
                else:
                    # Middle contributions - skip to avoid double counting
                    adjusted_length = 0
            else:
                # Normal case - single contribution or zero length
                adjusted_length = slice_length

            if not target_inside_block:
                # Normal case: Walk through the template.
                self.move_to_slice(target_slice_idx, adjusted_length)
            else:
                # {% block %} executes code elsewhere in the template but does
                # not move there. It's a bit like macro invocation.
                self.record_trace(adjusted_length, target_slice_idx)</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__3.1/src/sqlfluff/core/templaters/slicers/tracer.py has been edited.
